# a proof of theorem 5.1

we prove the following two lemmas before giving the proof of theorem 5.1.

lemma a.1 suppose that assumption 2.1 holds. let  $\{(x_k, y_k)\}$  be a sequence generated by algorithm 2 with parameter settings in (5.6). if  $\hat{\eta} > l_{11}$ , then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}\right) - l \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) \\ \leq - \left(\frac {\hat {\eta}}{2} - \frac {l _ {1 2} ^ {2} \hat {\rho}}{2}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \left(\frac {\hat {\mu}}{2} - \frac {1}{\hat {\rho}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \left(\hat {\mu} - \frac {1}{2 \hat {\rho}} - \frac {\dot {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2}\right) \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {a.1} \\ \end{array}
$$

proof the optimality condition for  $y_{k}$  in (5.5) implies that  $\forall y\in \mathcal{v}$  and  $\forall k\geq 1$

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \frac {1}{\hat {\rho}} \left(y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k}\right) - \partial g _ {1} \left(y _ {k + 1}\right), y - y _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \leq 0. \tag {a.2}
$$

by choosing  $y = y_{k}$  in (a.2), we have

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \frac {1}{\hat {\rho}} \left(y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k}\right) - \partial g _ {1} \left(y _ {k + 1}\right), y _ {k} - y _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \leq 0. \tag {a.3}
$$

on the other hand, by replacing  $k$  with  $k - 1$  and choosing  $y = y_{k + 1}$  in (a.2), we obtain

$$
\langle \nabla_ {y} f (x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}) - \frac {1}{\hat {\rho}} (y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1}) - \partial g _ {1} (y _ {k}), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \rangle \leq 0, \tag {a.4}
$$

which, in view of the fact that  $f(x,y)$  is  $\hat{\mu}$ -strongly concave w.r.t.  $y$  for any given  $x \in \mathcal{x}$  and  $g_{1}(y)$  is convex, then implies that

$$
\begin{array}{l} l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}\right) - l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) \\ \leq \langle \nabla_ {y} f (x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}) - \partial g _ {1} (y _ {k}), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \rangle - \frac {\hat {\mu}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle + \frac {1}{\hat {\rho}} \left\langle y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1}, y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle - \frac {\hat {\mu}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2}. \tag {a.5} \\ \end{array}
$$

denoting  $v_{k + 1} \coloneqq (y_{k + 1} - y_k) - (y_k - y_{k - 1})$ , we can write the first inner product term in the r.h.s. of (a.5) as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle \\ = \left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle + \left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), v _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \\ + \left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \right\rangle . \tag {a.6} \\ \end{array}
$$

next, we estimate the three terms in the right hand side of (a.6) respectively. by assumption 2.1 and the cauchy-schwarz inequality, we can bound the first two terms according to

$$
\langle \nabla_ {y} f (x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}) - \nabla_ {y} f (x _ {k}, y _ {k}), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \rangle \leq \frac {l _ {1 2} ^ {2} \hat {\rho}}{2} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 \hat {\rho}} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2}, \tag {a.7}
$$

and

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), v _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \leq \frac {\hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2} \left\| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 \hat {\rho}} \left\| v _ {k + 1} \right\| ^ {2}. \tag {a.8}
$$

for the third term, by  $\hat{\mu}$ -strongly-concavity of  $f$  with respect to  $y$ ,

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \right\rangle \leq - \hat {\mu} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {a.9}
$$

moreover, it can be easily checked that

$$
\langle y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1}, y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \rangle = \frac {1}{2} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \| v _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {a.10}
$$

plugging (a.6)-(a.10) into (a.5) and rearranging the terms, we conclude that

$$
\begin{array}{l} l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}\right) - l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) \\ \leq \frac {l _ {1 2} ^ {2} \hat {\rho}}{2} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} - (\hat {\mu} - \frac {1}{2 \hat {\rho}} - \frac {\hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2}) \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} - (\frac {\hat {\mu}}{2} - \frac {1}{\hat {\rho}}) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2}. \tag {a.11} \\ \end{array}
$$

the proof is completed by combining (a.11) with (5.7) in lemma 5.1.

lemma a.2 suppose that assumption 2.1 holds. let  $\{(x_k, y_k)\}$  be a sequence generated by algorithm 2 with parameter settings in (5.6). also let us denote

$$
l _ {k + 1} := l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}\right), s _ {k + 1} := \frac {2}{\hat {\rho} ^ {2} \hat {\mu}} \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2},
$$

$$
f _ {k + 1} := l _ {k + 1} + s _ {k + 1} - (\hat {\mu} + \frac {7}{2 \hat {\rho}} - \frac {\hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {2 l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{\hat {\mu}}) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2}.
$$

if  $\hat{\eta} > l_{11}$ , then

$$
f _ {k + 1} - f _ {k} \leq - \left(\frac {\hat {\eta}}{2} - \frac {\hat {\rho} l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {2 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\hat {\rho} \hat {\mu} ^ {2}}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \left(\frac {3 \hat {\mu} - \hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2} + \frac {\hat {\mu} - 4 \hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2 \hat {\rho} \hat {\mu}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2}. \tag {a.12}
$$

proof first by (a.3) and (a.4), we have

$$
\frac {1}{\hat {\rho}} \left\langle v _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle \leq \left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right) - \partial g _ {1} \left(y _ {k + 1}\right) + \partial g _ {1} \left(y _ {k}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle , \tag {a.13}
$$

by the  $\langle \partial g_1(y_{k + 1}) - \partial g_1(y_k),y_{k + 1} - y_k\rangle \geq 0$ , and together with (a.6) then imply that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{\hat {\rho}} \langle v _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \rangle \leq \langle \nabla_ {y} f (x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}) - \nabla_ {y} f (x _ {k}, y _ {k}), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \rangle \\ + \left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), v _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \\ + \left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \right\rangle . \tag {a.14} \\ \end{array}
$$

similar to (a.7), we can easily see that

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle \leq \frac {l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2 \hat {\mu}} \left\| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {\mu}}{2} \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2}. \tag {a.15}
$$

by plugging (a.8), (a.9), (a.15) into (a.14), and using the identity  $\frac{1}{\hat{\rho}}\langle v_{k + 1},y_{k + 1} - y_k\rangle = \frac{1}{2\hat{\rho}}\| y_{k + 1} - y_k\|^2 +\frac{1}{2\hat{\rho}}\| v_{k + 1}\|^2 -\frac{1}{2\hat{\rho}}\| y_k - y_{k - 1}\|^2$  we conclude that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{2 \hat {\rho}} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 \hat {\rho}} \| v _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2 \hat {\rho}} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2 \hat {\mu}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {\mu}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 \hat {\rho}} \| v _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} - \hat {\mu} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {a.16} \\ \end{array}
$$

rearranging the terms of (a.16), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{2 \hat {\rho}} \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2 \hat {\rho}} \left\| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \right\| ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2 \hat {\mu}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {\mu}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \left(\hat {\mu} - \frac {\hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2}\right) \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {a.17} \\ \end{array}
$$

multiplying  $\frac{4}{\hat{\rho}\hat{\mu}}$  on both sides of (a.17) and using the definition of  $s_{k + 1}$ , we obtain

$$
s _ {k + 1} - s _ {k} \leq \frac {2 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\hat {\mu} ^ {2} \hat {\rho}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{\hat {\rho}} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \left(\frac {4}{\hat {\rho}} - \frac {2 l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{\hat {\mu}}\right) \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2}.
$$

it then follows from (a.1) in lemma a.1 and the definition of  $f_{k}$  that

$$
f _ {k + 1} - f _ {k} \leq - \left(\frac {\hat {\eta}}{2} - \frac {\hat {\rho} l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {2 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\hat {\rho} \hat {\mu} ^ {2}}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \left(\frac {3 \hat {\mu} - \hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2} + \frac {\hat {\mu} - 4 \hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2 \hat {\rho} \hat {\mu}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2}.
$$

# the proof of theorem 5.1

proof noting that (5.4) is equivalent to  $x_{k + 1}^{(i)} = \mathrm{prox}_{h_i,\mathcal{x}_i}^{\hat{\eta}}\left(x_k^{(i)} - \frac{1}{\hat{\eta}}\nabla_{x^{(i)}}f\left(v_{k + 1}^{(i)},y_k\right)\right)$ , we immediately obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \left(\nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k}\right) _ {x ^ {(i)}} \| \\ \leq \hat {\eta} \| \operatorname {p r o x} _ {h _ {i}, \mathcal {x} _ {i}} ^ {\hat {\eta}} \left(x _ {k} ^ {(i)} - \frac {1}{\hat {\eta}} \nabla_ {x ^ {(i)}} f \left(v _ {k + 1} ^ {(i)}, y _ {k}\right)\right) - \operatorname {p r o x} _ {h _ {i}, \mathcal {x} _ {i}} ^ {\hat {\eta}} \left(x _ {k} ^ {(i)} - \frac {1}{\hat {\eta}} \nabla_ {x ^ {(i)}} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right)\right) \| \\ + \hat {\eta} \| x _ {k + 1} ^ {(i)} - x _ {k} ^ {(i)} \| \\ \leq \hat {\eta} \| x _ {k + 1} ^ {(i)} - x _ {k} ^ {(i)} \| + \| \nabla_ {x ^ {(i)}} f (v _ {k + 1} ^ {(i)}, y _ {k}) - \nabla_ {x ^ {(i)}} f (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) \| \\ \leq \hat {\eta} \| x _ {k + 1} ^ {(i)} - x _ {k} ^ {(i)} \| + l _ {1 1} \| v _ {k + 1} ^ {(i)} - x _ {k} \| \\ \leq \left(\hat {\eta} + l _ {1 1}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \|, \tag {a.18} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality holds by the nonexpansiveness of the proximal operator  $\mathrm{prox}_{h_i,\mathcal{x}_i}^{\hat{\eta}}$ , the last inequality holds since  $\| x_{k + 1}^{(i)} - x_k^{(i)}\| \leq \| x_{k + 1} - x_k\|$ ,  $\| v_{k + 1}^{(i)} - x_k\| \leq \| x_{k + 1} - x_k\|$  by the definition of  $x^{(i)}$  and  $v_{k + 1}^{(i)}$ . on the other hand, since (5.5) is equivalent to  $y_{k + 1} = \mathrm{prox}_{g_1,\mathcal{y}}^{1 / \hat{\rho}}(y_k + \hat{\rho}\nabla_yf(x_{k + 1},y_k))$ , we conclude from the triangle inequality that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| (\nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k}) _ {y} \| \\ \leq \frac {1}{\hat {\rho}} \| \operatorname {p r o x} _ {g _ {1}, \mathcal {y}} ^ {1 / \hat {\rho}} \left(y _ {k} + \hat {\rho} \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right)\right) - \operatorname {p r o x} _ {g _ {1}, \mathcal {y}} ^ {1 / \hat {\rho}} \left(y _ {k} + \hat {\rho} \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right)\right) \| \\ + \frac {1}{\hat {\rho}} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| \\ \leq \frac {1}{\hat {\rho}} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| + l _ {1 2} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \|. \tag {a.19} \\ \end{array}
$$

by combining (a.18)-(a.19), and using cauchy-schwarz inequality, we obtain

$$
\left\| \nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \leq \left(k _ {1} (\hat {\eta} + l _ {1 1}) ^ {2} + 2 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}\right) \left\| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{\hat {\rho} ^ {2}} \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2}. \tag {a.20}
$$

observing that  $r_1 > 0$ . multiplying both sides of (a.20) by  $r_1$ , and using (a.12) in lemma a.2, we have

$$
r _ {1} \| \nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq f _ {k} - f _ {k + 1}. \tag {a.21}
$$

summing up the above inequalities from  $k = 1$  to  $k = \mathcal{t}(\varepsilon)$  and using the definition of  $l_{1}$ , we obtain

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon)} r _ {1} \left\| \nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \leq f _ {1} - f _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) + 1} \leq l _ {1} - f _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) + 1}. \tag {a.22}
$$

note that by the definition of  $f_{k + 1}$  in lemma a.2, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} f _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) + 1} = l _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) + 1} + s _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) + 1} - (\hat {\mu} + \frac {7}{2 \hat {\rho}} - \frac {\hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {2 l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{\hat {\mu}}) \| y _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) + 1} - y _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon)} \| ^ {2} \\ \geq \underline {{l}} - (\hat {\mu} + \frac {7}{2 \hat {\rho}} - \frac {\hat {\rho} l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {2 l _ {2 2} ^ {2}}{\hat {\mu}}) \delta_ {y} ^ {2} = \underline {{l}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the inequality follows from the definitions of  $\underline{l}$  and  $\delta_y$ , and the facts that  $s_k \geq 0$  ( $\forall k \geq 1$ ) and  $\hat{\mu} + \frac{7}{2\hat{\rho}} - \frac{\hat{\rho}l_{22}^2}{2} - \frac{2l_{22}^2}{\hat{\mu}} \geq 0$  due to the selection of  $\hat{\rho}$ . we then conclude from (a.22) that

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon)} r _ {1} \left\| \nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \leq l _ {1} - f _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) + 1} \leq l _ {1} - \underline {{l}},
$$

which, in view of the definition of  $\mathcal{t}(\varepsilon)$ , implies that  $\varepsilon^2 \leq (l_1 - \underline{l}) / (\mathcal{t}(\varepsilon) \cdot r_1)$  or equivalently,  $\mathcal{t}(\varepsilon) \leq (l_1 - \underline{l}) / (r_1 \varepsilon^2)$ .

# b proof of theorem 5.2

we prove the following two lemmas before giving the proof of theorem 5.2.

lemma b.1 suppose that assumptions 2.1 and 5.1 hold. let  $\{(x_k, y_k)\}$  be a sequence generated by algorithm 2 with parameter settings in (5.11). if  $\forall k, \vartheta_k > l_{11}$  and  $\xi \leq \frac{2}{l_{22}' + \hat{\varepsilon}_1}$ , then

$$
\begin{array}{l} l (x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}) - l (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) \\ \leq - \left(\iota + \frac {\vartheta_ {k}}{2} - \frac {\xi l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{\xi} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{2} \left(\left\| y _ {k + 1} \right\| ^ {2} - \left\| y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2}\right). \tag {b.1} \\ \end{array}
$$

proof the optimality condition for  $y_{k}$  in (5.5) implies that,  $\forall y\in \mathcal{v},\forall k\geq 1$

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k} \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \frac {1}{\xi} \left(y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k}\right) - \partial g _ {1} \left(y _ {k + 1}\right), y - y _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \leq 0. \tag {b.2}
$$

by choosing  $y = y_{k}$  in (b.2), we have

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k} \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \frac {1}{\xi} \left(y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k}\right) - \partial g _ {1} \left(y _ {k + 1}\right), y _ {k} - y _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \leq 0. \tag {b.3}
$$

on the other hand, by replacing  $k$  with  $k - 1$  and choosing  $y = y_{k + 1}$  in (b.2), we obtain

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right) - \frac {1}{\xi} \left(y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1}\right) - \partial g _ {1} \left(y _ {k}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle \leq 0. \tag {b.4}
$$

the concavity of  $\bar{f}_k(x_{k + 1},y)$  w.r.t.  $y$  together with (b.4) and  $g_{1}(y)$  is convex, then imply that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \bar {l} _ {k} \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}\right) - \bar {l} _ {k} \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) \\ \leq \left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k} \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {x} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle + \frac {1}{\xi} \left\langle y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1}, y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle \\ = \left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k} \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle + \left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), v _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \\ + \left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \right\rangle + \frac {1}{\xi} \left\langle y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1}, y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle , \tag {b.5} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $v_{k + 1} = y_{k + 1} - y_k - (y_k - y_{k - 1})$ . we now provide bounds on the inner product terms of (b.5). firstly, by the definition of  $\bar{f}_k(x_{k + 1},y_k)$  and  $\bar{f}_{k - 1}(x_k,y_k)$ , assumptions 2.1 and 5.1, and the cauchy-schwarz inequality, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k} (x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} (x _ {k}, y _ {k}), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \rangle \\ = \left\langle \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle - \left(\hat {c} _ {k} - \hat {c} _ {k - 1}\right) \left\langle y _ {k}, y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle \\ \leq \frac {\xi l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k} - \hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{2} (\| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} - \| y _ {k} \| ^ {2}) \\ + \frac {\hat {c} _ {k} - \hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {\xi l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k} - \hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{2} \left(\| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} - \| y _ {k} \| ^ {2}\right). \tag {b.6} \\ \end{array}
$$

secondly, by the cauchy-schwarz inequality,

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), v _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \leq \frac {\xi}{2} \| \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right) \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| v _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {b.7}
$$

thirdly, it follows from the concavity of  $\bar{f}_{k - 1}(x_k,y)$  w.r.t.  $y$  that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} (x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}), y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \rangle \\ \leq - \frac {1}{l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime} + \hat {c} _ {k - 1}} \| \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} (x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}) \| ^ {2} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1} l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime}}{l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime} + \hat {c} _ {k - 1}} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq - \frac {1}{l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime} + \hat {c} _ {k - 1}} \| \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right) \| ^ {2}. \tag {b.8} \\ \end{array}
$$

also observe that

$$
\frac {1}{\xi} \left\langle y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k}, y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \right\rangle = \frac {1}{2 \xi} \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 \xi} \left\| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \right\| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2 \xi} \left\| v _ {k + 1} \right\| ^ {2}. \tag {b.9}
$$

plugging (b.6)-(b.9) into (b.5), and using the definition of  $\bar{l}_k(x_{k + 1},y_{k + 1})$  and  $\bar{l}_k(x_{k + 1},y_k)$  and the assumption  $\xi \frac{1}{2}\leq \frac{1}{l_{22}^{\prime} + \dot{\varepsilon}_{1}}$ , we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}\right) - l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) \leq \frac {\xi l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2} \left\| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{\xi} \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{2} (\| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} - \| y _ {k} \| ^ {2}). \tag {b.10} \\ \end{array}
$$

the result in (b.1) the follows by adding (b.10) and (5.12) in lemma 5.2.

lemma b.2 suppose that assumptions 2.1 and 5.1 hold. let  $\{(x_k, y_k)\}$  be a sequence generated by algorithm 2 with parameter settings in (5.11). also let us denote

$$
\mathcal {s} _ {k + 1} := \frac {8}{\xi^ {2} \hat {c} _ {k + 1}} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {8}{\xi} \left(1 - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k}}{\hat {c} _ {k + 1}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2},
$$

$$
\mathcal {f} _ {k + 1} := l (x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}) + \mathcal {s} _ {k + 1} - \frac {1 5}{2 \xi} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}.
$$

if

$$
\vartheta_ {k} > l _ {1 1}, \frac {1}{\hat {c} _ {k + 1}} - \frac {1}{\hat {c} _ {k}} \leq \frac {\xi}{5}, \xi \leq \frac {2}{l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime} + \hat {c} _ {1}}, \tag {b.11}
$$

then  $\forall k\geq 2$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {f} _ {k + 1} - \mathcal {f} _ {k} \leq - \left(\iota + \frac {\vartheta_ {k}}{2} - \frac {\xi l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {1 6 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\xi \hat {c} _ {k} ^ {2}}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1} - \hat {c} _ {k}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} (b.12) \\ - \frac {9}{1 0 \xi} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {8}{\xi} \left(\frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{\hat {c} _ {k}} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k}}{\hat {c} _ {k + 1}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}. (b.13) \\ \end{array}
$$

proof by (b.3) and (b.4), we have

$$
\frac {1}{\xi} \langle v _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \rangle \leq \left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k} \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right) - \partial g _ {1} \left(y _ {k + 1}\right) + \partial g _ {1} \left(y _ {k}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle . \tag {b.14}
$$

by the  $\langle \partial g_1(y_{k + 1}) - \partial g_1(y_k),y_{k + 1} - y_k\rangle \geq 0$ , similar to (b.5) in lemma b.1, (b.14) can be rewritten as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{\xi} \langle v _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \rangle \\ \leq \left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k} \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right), y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\rangle + \left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), v _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \\ + \left\langle \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}\right), y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \right\rangle . \\ \end{array}
$$

using an argument similar to the proof of (b.6)-(b.9), the concavity of  $\bar{f}_{k - 1}(x_k,y)$  w.r.t.  $y$ , and the cauchy-schwarz inequality, we conclude from the above inequality that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| v _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2 a _ {k}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {a _ {k}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k} - \hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{2} (\| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} - \| y _ {k} \| ^ {2}) \\ + \frac {\xi}{2} \| \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} (x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}) \| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime} + \hat {c} _ {k - 1}} \| \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) - \nabla_ {y} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} (x _ {k}, y _ {k - 1}) \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| v _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1} l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime}}{l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime} + \hat {c} _ {k - 1}} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {c} _ {k} - \hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2}, \tag {b.15} \\ \end{array}
$$

for any  $a_{k} > 0$ . observing by  $\hat{c}_1 \leq l_{22}'$ , and assumption 5.1, we have

$$
- \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1} l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime}}{\hat {c} _ {k - 1} + l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime}} \leq - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1} l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime}}{2 l _ {2 2} ^ {\prime}} = - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{2} \leq - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k}}{2}.
$$

combining  $\xi \leq \frac{2}{l_{22}^{\prime} + \hat{c}_{1}}$  and rearranging the terms in (b.15), we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {c} _ {k} - \hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {1}{2 \xi} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {c} _ {k} - \hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{2} \| y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2 a _ {k}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {a _ {k}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k}}{2} \left\| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \right\| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

by multiplying  $\frac{16}{\xi\hat{c}_k}$  on both sides of the above inequality, we then obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {8}{\xi^ {2} \hat {c} _ {k}} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {8}{\xi} \left(1 - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{\hat {c} _ {k}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {8}{\xi^ {2} \hat {c} _ {k}} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} + \frac {8}{\xi} (1 - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{\hat {c} _ {k}}) \| y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {8 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\xi \hat {c} _ {k} a _ {k}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {8 a _ {k}}{\xi \hat {c} _ {k}} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \frac {8}{\xi} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {b.16} \\ \end{array}
$$

setting  $a_k = \frac{\hat{c}_k}{2}$  in the above inequality, and using the definition of  $s_{k+1}$  and (b.11), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {s} _ {k + 1} - \mathcal {s} _ {k} \leq \frac {8}{\xi} \left(\frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{\hat {c} _ {k}} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k}}{\hat {c} _ {k + 1}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1 6 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\xi \hat {c} _ {k} ^ {2}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {2 8}{5 \xi} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ - \frac {8}{\xi} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {b.17} \\ \end{array}
$$

combining (b.17) and (b.1) in lemma b.1, and using the definition of  $\mathcal{f}_{k + 1}$ , we conclude

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {f} _ {k + 1} - \mathcal {f} _ {k} \leq - \left(\iota + \frac {\vartheta_ {k}}{2} - \frac {\xi l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {1 6 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\xi \hat {c} _ {k} ^ {2}}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1} - \hat {c} _ {k}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} \\ - \frac {9}{1 0 \xi} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {8}{\xi} \left(\frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{\hat {c} _ {k}} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k}}{\hat {c} _ {k + 1}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

# the proof of theorem 5.2

proof by  $\xi \leq \frac{1}{10l_{22}}$  and  $\hat{c}_k = \frac{19}{20\xi k^{1/4}}, \forall k \geq 1$ , let us denote  $\vartheta_k = \xi l_{12}^2 + \frac{16\tau l_{12}^2}{\xi\hat{c}_k^2} - 2\iota$ ,  $\alpha_k = \frac{8(\tau - 2)l_{12}^2}{\xi\hat{c}_k^2}$ , we can easily see that the relations in (b.11) are satisfied. it follows from the selection of  $\vartheta_k$  and  $\alpha_k$  that

$$
\iota + \frac {\vartheta_ {k}}{2} - \frac {\xi l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {1 6 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\xi \hat {c} _ {k} ^ {2}} = \alpha_ {k}.
$$

this observation, in view of lemma b.2, then immediately implies that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \alpha_ {k} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {9}{1 0 \xi} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq \mathcal {f} _ {k} - \mathcal {f} _ {k + 1} + \frac {8}{\xi} \left(\frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{\hat {c} _ {k}} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k}}{\hat {c} _ {k + 1}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1} - \hat {c} _ {k}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {b.18} \\ \end{array}
$$

we can easily check from the definition of  $\bar{f}_k(x_k,y_k)$  that

$$
\left\| \nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k} \right\| - \left\| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \right\| \leq \hat {c} _ {k} \| y _ {k} \|.
$$

by replacing  $f$  with  $\bar{f}_k$ ,  $\hat{\eta}$  with  $\vartheta_k + \iota$ , similar to (a.18) and (a.19), we immediately obtain that

$$
\left\| \left(\nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k}\right) _ {x ^ {(i)}} \right\| \leq \left(\vartheta_ {k} + \iota + l _ {1 1}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \|, \tag {b.19}
$$

and

$$
\left\| \left(\nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k}\right) _ {y} \right\| \leq \frac {1}{\xi} \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| + l _ {1 2} \left\| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\|. \tag {b.20}
$$

combining (b.19) and (b.20), and using the cauchy-schwarz inequality, we have

$$
\left\| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \leq \left(k _ {1} \left(\vartheta_ {k} + \iota + l _ {1 1}\right) ^ {2} + 2 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}\right) \left\| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{\xi^ {2}} \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2}. \tag {b.21}
$$

since both  $\alpha_{k}$  and  $\vartheta_{k}$  are in the same order when  $k$  becomes large enough, it then follows from the definition of  $r_1$  that  $\forall k\geq 1$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {k _ {1} \left(\vartheta_ {k} + \iota + l _ {1 1}\right) ^ {2} + 2 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\alpha_ {k} ^ {2}} = \frac {k _ {1} \left(\xi l _ {1 2} ^ {2} + \frac {1 6 \tau l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\xi (\bar {\epsilon} _ {k}) ^ {2}} - \iota + l _ {1 1}\right) ^ {2} + 2 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\alpha_ {k} ^ {2}} \\ \leq \frac {2 k _ {1} \left(\frac {1 6 \tau l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\xi (\hat {c} _ {k}) ^ {2}}\right) ^ {2} + 2 k _ {1} \left(\xi l _ {1 2} ^ {2} - \iota + l _ {1 1}\right) ^ {2} + 2 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}}{\alpha_ {k} ^ {2}} \\ = \frac {8 k _ {1} \tau^ {2}}{(\tau - 2) ^ {2}} + \frac {1 9 ^ {4} \left(2 k _ {1} \left(\xi l _ {1 2} ^ {2} - \iota + l _ {1 1}\right) ^ {2} + 2 l _ {1 2} ^ {2}\right)}{6 4 \cdot 2 0 ^ {4} \xi^ {2} (\tau - 2) ^ {2} l _ {1 2} ^ {4} k} \\ \leq r _ {1} \tag {b.22} \\ \end{array}
$$

combining the previous two inequalities in (b.22) and (b.21), we obtain

$$
\left\| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \leq r _ {1} \left(\alpha_ {k}\right) ^ {2} \left\| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{\xi^ {2}} \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2}. \tag {b.23}
$$

denote  $r_k^{(2)} = \frac{1}{\max\left\{r_1\alpha_k,\frac{20}{9\xi}\right\}}$ . by multiplying  $r_k^{(2)}$  on the both sides of (b.23), and using (b.18), we have

$$
r _ {k} ^ {(2)} \| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq \mathcal {f} _ {k} - \mathcal {f} _ {k + 1} + \frac {8}{\xi} \left(\frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1}}{\hat {c} _ {k}} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {k}}{\hat {c} _ {k + 1}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {c} _ {k - 1} - \hat {c} _ {k}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}, \tag {b.24}
$$

where the last inequality follows since  $r_k^{(2)}$  is a decreasing sequence. denoting

$$
\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon) := \min  \left\{k \mid \| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) \| \leq \frac {\varepsilon}{2}, k \geq 2 \right\},
$$

summing both sides of (b.24) from  $k = 2$  to  $k = \widetilde{\mathcal{t}} (\varepsilon)$ , we then obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)} r _ {k} ^ {(2)} \left\| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \\ \leq \mathcal {f} _ {2} - \mathcal {f} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)} + \frac {8}{\xi} \left(\frac {\hat {c} _ {1}}{\hat {c} _ {2}} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)}}{\hat {c} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon) + 1}}\right) \hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {c} _ {1} - \hat {c} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)}}{2} \hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2} \\ \leq \mathcal {f} _ {2} - \mathcal {f} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)} + \frac {8 \hat {c} _ {1}}{\xi \hat {c} _ {2}} \hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {c} _ {1}}{2} \hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2} \\ = \mathcal {f} _ {2} - \mathcal {f} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)} + \left(8 \cdot 2 ^ {1 / 4} + \frac {1 9}{4 0}\right) \frac {\hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2}}{\xi}. \tag {b.25} \\ \end{array}
$$

note that by the definition of  $\mathcal{f}_{k + 1}$  in lemma b.2, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {f} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)} \geq \underline {{l}} - \frac {8 \hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2}}{\xi} \left(1 + \frac {1}{\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)}\right) ^ {1 / 4} - \frac {1 5 \hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2}}{\xi} - \frac {\hat {c} _ {1} \hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2}}{2} \\ = \underline {{l}} - \left(2 ^ {1 3 / 4} + 1 5 + \frac {1 9}{4 0}\right) \frac {\hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2}}{\xi} = \underline {{\mathcal {l}}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\underline{l} \coloneqq \min_{(x,y) \in \mathcal{x} \times \mathcal{y}} l(x,y)$ . by the definition of  $\mathcal{l}_2$ , we then conclude from (b.25) that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)} r _ {k} ^ {(2)} \| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq \mathcal {f} _ {2} - \underline {{\mathcal {l}}} + \left(8 \cdot 2 ^ {1 / 4} + \frac {1 9}{4 0}\right) \frac {\hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2}}{\xi} \\ \leq \mathcal {l} _ {2} - \underline {{\mathcal {l}}} + \left(8 \cdot 2 ^ {1 / 4} + \frac {1 9}{4 0}\right) \frac {\hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {2}}{\xi} = r _ {2}. \tag {b.26} \\ \end{array}
$$

we can see from the selection of  $r_3$  that  $r_3 = \max \{r_1, \frac{20}{9\xi\alpha_2}\}$ . observe that  $\alpha_k$  is an increasing sequence, when  $k \geq 2$ , we have that  $r_3 \geq \max \{r_1, \frac{20}{9\xi\alpha_k}\}$ , which implies that  $r_k^{(2)} \geq \frac{1}{r_3\alpha_k}$ , by multiplying  $r_3$  on the both sides of (b.26), and combining the definition of  $r_2$ , we have  $\sum_{k=2}^{\bar{t}(\varepsilon)} \frac{1}{\alpha_k} \| \nabla \bar{\mathcal{g}}_k \|^2 \leq r_2 r_3$ , which, by the definition of  $\bar{t}(\varepsilon)$ , implies that

$$
\frac {\varepsilon^ {2}}{4} \leq \frac {r _ {2} r _ {3}}{\sum_ {k = 2} ^ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)} \frac {1}{\alpha_ {k}}}. \tag {b.27}
$$

note that when  $\hat{c}_k = \frac{19}{20\xi k^{1 / 4}}$ ,  $\alpha_{k} = \frac{2\cdot 40^{2}\xi(\tau - 2)l_{12}^{2}\sqrt{k}}{19^{2}}$ . by using the fact  $\sum_{k = 2}^{\bar{t} (\varepsilon)}1 / \sqrt{k}\geq \sqrt{\bar{t}(\varepsilon)} -1$  and (b.27), we conclude  $\frac{\varepsilon^2}{4}\leq \frac{2\cdot 40^2\xi(\tau - 2)l_{12}^2r_2r_3}{19^2\left(\sqrt{\bar{t}(\varepsilon)} - 1\right)}$  or equivalently,

$$
\bar {t} (\varepsilon) \leq \left(\frac {2 \cdot 8 0 ^ {2} \xi (\tau - 2) l _ {1 2} ^ {2} r _ {2} r _ {3}}{1 9 ^ {2} \varepsilon^ {2}} + 1\right) ^ {2}.
$$

on the other hand, if  $k \geq \frac{19^4\hat{\delta}_y^4}{10^4\xi^4\varepsilon^4}$ , then  $c_k = \frac{19}{20\xi k^{1/4}} \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2\hat{\delta}_y}$ . this inequality together with the definition of  $\hat{\delta}_y$  then imply that  $\hat{c}_k \| y_k \| \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2}$ . therefore, there exists a

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) \leq \max  \left(\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon), \frac {1 9 ^ {4} \hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {4}}{1 0 ^ {4} \xi^ {4} \varepsilon^ {4}}\right) \\ \leq \max \left(\left(\frac {2 \cdot 8 0 ^ {2} \xi (\tau - 2) l _ {1 2} ^ {2} r _ {2} r _ {3}}{1 9 ^ {2} \varepsilon^ {2}} + 1\right) ^ {2}, \frac {1 9 ^ {4} \hat {\delta} _ {y} ^ {4}}{1 0 ^ {4} \xi^ {4} \varepsilon^ {4}}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

such that  $\| \nabla \mathcal{g}_k\| \leq \| \nabla \bar{\mathcal{g}}_k\| +\hat{c}_k\| y_k\| \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2} +\frac{\varepsilon}{2} = \varepsilon$

# c proof of theorem 5.3

we prove the following two lemmas before giving the proof of theorem 5.3.

lemma c.1 suppose that assumption 2.1 holds. let  $\{(x_k, y_k)\}$  be a sequence generated by algorithm 2 with parameter settings in (5.13). if  $\bar{\xi} > l_{22}$ , then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}\right) - l \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) \\ \geq \frac {\bar {\xi}}{2} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \frac {l _ {2 1} ^ {2} \varrho}{2} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} + (\hat {\theta} - \frac {1}{2 \varrho} - \frac {\varrho l _ {1 1} ^ {2}}{2}) \| x _ {k} - x _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} \\ + \left(\frac {\hat {\theta}}{2} - \frac {1}{\varrho}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2}. \tag {c.1} \\ \end{array}
$$

proof similar to (a.2)-(a.4) in the proof of lemma a.1, by the optimality condition for  $x_{k}$  in (5.4) implies that  $\forall x \in \mathcal{x}$  and  $\forall k \geq 1$ ,

$$
\langle \nabla_ {x} f (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) + \partial h _ {1} (x _ {k + 1}) + \frac {1}{\varrho} (x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k}), x - x _ {k + 1} \rangle \geq 0, \tag {c.2}
$$

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {x} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) + \partial h _ {1} \left(x _ {k + 1}\right) + \frac {1}{\varrho} \left(x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k}\right), x _ {k} - x _ {k + 1} \right\rangle \geq 0, \tag {c.3}
$$

$$
\left\langle \nabla_ {x} f \left(x _ {k - 1}, y _ {k - 1}\right) + \partial h _ {1} \left(x _ {k}\right) + \frac {1}{\varrho} \left(x _ {k} - x _ {k - 1}\right), x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\rangle \geq 0, \tag {c.4}
$$

which, in view of the fact that  $f(x,y)$  is  $\hat{\theta}$ -strongly convex w.r.t.  $x$  for any given  $y \in \mathcal{v}$  and  $h_1(x)$  is convex, then implies that

$$
\begin{array}{l} l (x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}) - l (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) \geq \langle \nabla_ {x} f (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) + \partial h _ {1} (x _ {k}), x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \rangle + \frac {\hat {\theta}}{2} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \geq \langle \nabla_ {x} f (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) - \nabla_ {x} f (x _ {k - 1}, y _ {k - 1}), x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \rangle \\ - \frac {1}{\varrho} \left\langle x _ {k} - x _ {k - 1}, x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\rangle + \frac {\hat {\theta}}{2} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2}. \tag {c.5} \\ \end{array}
$$

the rest proof is the same with that of lemma 4.1 except replacing  $\zeta$  by  $\varrho$  and  $\theta$  by  $\hat{\theta}$ . we omit the details here.

lemma c.2 suppose that assumption 2.1 holds. let  $\{(x_k, y_k)\}$  be a sequence generated by algorithm 2 with parameter settings in (5.13). denote

$$
l _ {k + 1} := l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}\right), \hat {s} _ {k + 1} := - \frac {2}{\varrho^ {2} \hat {\theta}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2},
$$

$$
\hat {f} _ {k + 1} := l _ {k + 1} + \hat {s} _ {k + 1} + (\hat {\theta} + \frac {7}{2 \varrho} - \frac {\varrho l _ {1 1} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {2 l _ {1 1} ^ {2}}{\hat {\theta}}) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} - (\frac {l _ {2 1} ^ {2} \varrho}{2} + \frac {2 l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{\hat {\theta} ^ {2} \varrho}) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2}.
$$

if  $\bar{\xi} > l_{22}$ , then  $\forall k \geq 1$ ,

$$
\hat {f} _ {k + 1} - \hat {f} _ {k} \geq \left(\frac {\bar {\xi}}{2} - \frac {\varrho l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {2 l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{\varrho \hat {\theta} ^ {2}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {3 \hat {\theta} - \varrho l _ {1 1} ^ {2}}{2} + \frac {\hat {\theta} - 4 \varrho l _ {1 1} ^ {2}}{2 \varrho \hat {\theta}}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2}. \tag {c.6}
$$

proof first by (c.3) and (c.4), we have

$$
\frac {1}{\varrho} \langle m _ {k + 1}, x _ {k} - x _ {k + 1} \rangle \geq \left\langle \nabla_ {x} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {x} f \left(x _ {k - 1}, y _ {k - 1}\right) + \partial h _ {1} \left(x _ {k + 1}\right) - \partial h _ {1} \left(x _ {k}\right), x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\rangle , \tag {c.7}
$$

where  $m_{k + 1}\coloneqq (x_{k + 1} - x_k) - (x_k - x_{k - 1})$ , together with  $\langle \partial h_1(x_{k + 1}) - \partial h_1(x_k),x_{k + 1} - x_k\rangle \geq 0$  then imply that

$$
\frac {1}{\varrho} \langle m _ {k + 1}, x _ {k} - x _ {k + 1} \rangle \geq \left\langle \nabla_ {x} f \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {x} f \left(x _ {k - 1}, y _ {k - 1}\right), x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\rangle . \tag {c.8}
$$

the rest proof is the same with that of lemma 4.2 except replacing  $\zeta$  by  $\underline{\varrho}$  and  $\theta$  by  $\hat{\theta}$ . we omit the details here.

# the proof of theorem 5.3

proof noting that (5.5) is equivalent to  $y_{k + 1}^{(j)} = \mathrm{prox}_{g_j,\mathcal{y}_j}^{\bar{\xi}}\left(y_k^{(j)} + \frac{1}{\bar{\xi}}\nabla_{y^{(j)}}f\left(x_{k + 1},w_{k + 1}^{(j)}\right)\right)$ , we immediately obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| (\nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k}) _ {x (j)} \| \\ \leq \bar {\xi} \| \operatorname {p r o x} _ {g _ {j}, \mathcal {y} _ {j}} ^ {\bar {\xi}} \left(y _ {k} ^ {(j)} + \frac {1}{\bar {\xi}} \nabla_ {y ^ {(j)}} f (x _ {k + 1}, w _ {k + 1} ^ {(j)})\right) - \operatorname {p r o x} _ {g _ {j}, \mathcal {y} _ {j}} ^ {\bar {\xi}} \left(y _ {k} ^ {(j)} + \frac {1}{\bar {\xi}} \nabla_ {y ^ {(j)}} f (x _ {k}, y _ {k})\right) \| \\ + \bar {\xi} \| y _ {k + 1} ^ {(j)} - y _ {k} ^ {(j)} \| \\ \leq \bar {\xi} \| y _ {k + 1} ^ {(j)} - y _ {k} ^ {(j)} \| + \| \nabla_ {y ^ {(j)}} f (x _ {k + 1}, w _ {k + 1} ^ {(j)}) - \nabla_ {y ^ {(j)}} f (x _ {k}, y _ {k}) \| \\ \leq \tilde {\xi} \| y _ {k + 1} ^ {(j)} - y _ {k} ^ {(j)} \| + l _ {1 2} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| + l _ {2 2} \| w _ {k + 1} ^ {(j)} - y _ {k} \| \\ \leq \left(\bar {\xi} + l _ {2 2}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| + l _ {1 2} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \|, \tag {c.9} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality holds by the nonexpansiveness of the proximal operator  $\mathrm{prox}_{g_j, y_j}^{\bar{\ell}}$ , the last inequality holds since  $\|y_{k+1}^{(j)} - y_k^{(j)}\| \leq \|y_{k+1} - y_k\|$ ,  $\|w_{k+1}^{(j)} - y_k\| \leq \|y_{k+1} - y_k\|$  by the definition of  $y^{(j)}$  and  $w_{k+1}^{(j)}$ . on the other hand, since (5.4) is equivalent to  $x_{k+1} = \mathrm{prox}_{\mathcal{x}, h_1}^{1/\varrho}(x_k - \varrho\nabla_x f(x_k, y_k))$ , we conclude from the triangle inequality that

$$
\left\| \left(\nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k}\right) _ {x} \right\| = \frac {1}{\varrho} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \|. \tag {c.10}
$$

by combining (c.9)-(c.10), and using cauchy-schwarz inequality, we obtain

$$
\left\| \nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \leq \left(2 k _ {2} (\bar {\xi} + l _ {2 2}) ^ {2}\right) \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {1}{\varrho^ {2}} + 2 k _ {2} l _ {1 2} ^ {2}\right) \left\| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\| ^ {2}, \tag {c.11}
$$

observing that  $\hat{r}_1 > 0$ . multiplying both sides of (c.11) by  $\hat{r}_1$ , and using (a.12) in lemma c.2, we have

$$
\hat {r} _ {1} \| \nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq \hat {f} _ {k + 1} - \hat {f} _ {k}. \tag {c.12}
$$

summing up the above inequalities from  $k = 1$  to  $k = \mathcal{t}(\varepsilon)$  and using the definition of  $\hat{l}_1$ , we obtain

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon)} \hat {r} _ {1} \| \nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq \hat {f} _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) + 1} - \hat {f} _ {1} \leq \hat {f} _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) + 1} - \hat {l} _ {1}. \tag {c.13}
$$

note that by the definition of  $\hat{f}_{k + 1}$  in lemma c.2, we have

$$
\hat {f} _ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) + 1} \leq \hat {l} + (\hat {\theta} + \frac {7}{2 \varrho} - \frac {\varrho l _ {1 1} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {2 l _ {1 1} ^ {2}}{\hat {\theta}}) \delta_ {x} ^ {2} = \bar {l}.
$$

we then conclude from (c.13) that

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon)} \hat {r} _ {1} \| \nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq \bar {l} - \hat {l} _ {1},
$$

which, in view of the definition of  $\mathcal{t}(\varepsilon)$ , implies that  $\varepsilon^2 \leq (\bar{l} - \hat{l}_1) / (\mathcal{t}(\varepsilon) \cdot \hat{r}_1)$  or equivalently,  $\mathcal{t}(\varepsilon) \leq (\bar{l} - \hat{l}_1) / (\hat{r}_1 \varepsilon^2)$ .

# d proof of theorem 5.4

we prove the following three lemmas before giving the proof of theorem 5.4.

lemma d.1 suppose that assumption 2.1 holds. let  $\{(x_k, y_k)\}$  be a sequence generated by algorithm 2 with parameter settings in (5.17), if  $\forall k, \bar{\vartheta}_k > l_{22}$ , we have

$$
l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}\right) - l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) \geq \left(\bar {\iota} + \frac {\bar {\vartheta} _ {k}}{2}\right) \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2}. \tag {d.1}
$$

proof the proof is the same with that of lemma 5.3 except replacing  $\xi_k^{(j)}$  by  $\bar{\iota} +\bar{\vartheta}_k$ . we omit the details here.

lemma d.2 suppose that assumption 2.1 and 5.2 hold. let  $\{(x_k, y_k)\}$  be a sequence generated by algorithm 2 with parameter settings in (5.17). if  $\forall k, \bar{\vartheta}_k > l_{22}$  and  $\bar{\varrho} \leq \frac{2}{l_{11}' + \hat{b}_1}$ , then

$$
\begin{array}{l} l \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}\right) - l \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) \\ \geq \left(\bar {\iota} + \frac {\bar {\vartheta} _ {k}}{2}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \frac {l _ {2 1} ^ {2} \bar {\varrho}}{2} \| y _ {k} - y _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{\bar {\varrho}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2 \bar {\varrho}} \| x _ {k} - x _ {k - 1} \| ^ {2} \\ - \frac {\hat {b} _ {k - 1}}{2} \left(\left\| x _ {k + 1} \right\| ^ {2} - \left\| x _ {k} \right\| ^ {2}\right). \tag {d.2} \\ \end{array}
$$

proof similar to (c.2)-(c.4) in the proof of lemma c.1, by replacing  $l$  with  $\bar{l}_k$ ,  $f(x_k,y_k)$  with  $\bar{f}_k$ ,  $\varrho$  with  $\bar{\varrho}$  respectively, and setting  $\hat{\theta} = 0$ , we obtain that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \bar {l} _ {k} \left(x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k}\right) - \bar {l} _ {k} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) \geq \left\langle \nabla_ {x} \bar {f} _ {k} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) + \partial h _ {1} \left(x _ {k}\right), x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\rangle \\ \geq \left\langle \nabla_ {x} \bar {f} _ {k} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {x} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k - 1}, y _ {k - 1}\right), x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\rangle \\ - \frac {1}{\bar {\varrho}} \left\langle x _ {k} - x _ {k - 1}, x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\rangle . \tag {d.3} \\ \end{array}
$$

the rest proof is the same with lemma 4.3 except replacing  $\bar{\zeta}$  by  $\bar{\varrho}$  and  $q_{k}$  by  $\hat{b}_k$ . we omit the details here.

lemma d.3 suppose that assumptions 2.1 and 5.2 hold. let  $\{(x_k, y_k)\}$  be a sequence generated by algorithm 2 with parameter settings in (5.17). denote

$$
\begin{array}{l} \hat {\mathcal {s}} _ {k + 1} := - \frac {8}{\bar {\varrho} ^ {2} \hat {b} _ {k + 1}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \frac {8}{\bar {\varrho}} \left(1 - \frac {\hat {b} _ {k}}{\hat {b} _ {k + 1}}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}, \\ \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {k + 1} := l (x _ {k + 1}, y _ {k + 1}) + \hat {\mathcal {s}} _ {k + 1} + \frac {1 5}{2 \bar {\varrho}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {b} _ {k}}{2} \| x _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} \\ - \left(\frac {\bar {\varrho} l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{2} + \frac {1 6 l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{\bar {\varrho} (\hat {b} _ {k + 1}) ^ {2}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

if

$$
\bar {\vartheta} _ {k} > l _ {2 2}, \frac {1}{\hat {b} _ {k + 1}} - \frac {1}{\hat {b} _ {k}} \leq \frac {\bar {\varrho}}{5}, \bar {\varrho} \leq \frac {2}{l _ {1 1} ^ {\prime} + \hat {b} _ {1}}, \tag {d.4}
$$

then  $\forall k\geq 1$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {k + 1} - \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {k} \\ \geq \left(\bar {\iota} + \frac {\bar {\vartheta} _ {k}}{2} - \frac {\bar {\varrho} l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {1 6 l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{\bar {\varrho} (\hat {b} _ {k + 1}) ^ {2}}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {b} _ {k} - \hat {b} _ {k - 1}}{2} \| x _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {9}{1 0 \bar {\varrho}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {8}{\bar {\varrho}} \left(\frac {\hat {b} _ {k}}{\hat {b} _ {k + 1}} - \frac {\hat {b} _ {k - 1}}{\hat {b} _ {k}}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

proof similar to (c.7) in the proof of lemma c.2, by replacing  $f(x_{k},y_{k})$  with  $\bar{f}_k$ ,  $\varrho$  with  $\bar{\varrho}$  respectively, we have

$$
\frac {1}{\bar {\varrho}} \langle m _ {k + 1}, x _ {k} - x _ {k + 1} \rangle \geq \left\langle \nabla_ {x} \bar {f} _ {k} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) - \nabla_ {x} \bar {f} _ {k - 1} \left(x _ {k - 1}, y _ {k - 1}\right), x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\rangle ,
$$

the rest proof is the same with lemma 4.4 except replacing  $\bar{\zeta}$  by  $\bar{\varrho}$  and  $q_{k}$  by  $\hat{b}_k$ . we omit the details here.

# the proof of theorem 5.4

proof by  $\bar{\varrho} \leq \frac{1}{10l_{11}}, \hat{b}_k = \frac{19}{20\bar{\varrho}k^{1/4}}, \forall k \geq 1$ , let us denote  $\bar{\vartheta}_k = \bar{\varrho}l_{21}^2 + \frac{16\tau l_{21}^2}{\bar{\varrho}(\hat{b}_{k+1})^2} - 2\bar{\iota}$ ,  $p_k = \frac{8(\tau-2)l_{21}^2}{\bar{\varrho}(\hat{b}_{k+1})^2}$ , it can be easily checked that the relations in (d.4) are satisfied. it follows from the selection of  $\bar{\vartheta}_k$  and  $p_k$  that

$$
\bar {\iota} + \frac {\bar {\vartheta} _ {k}}{2} - \frac {\bar {\varrho} l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{2} - \frac {1 6 l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{\bar {\varrho} (\hat {b} _ {k + 1}) ^ {2}} = p _ {k}.
$$

this observation, in view of lemma d.3, then immediately implies that

$$
\begin{array}{l} p _ {k} \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {9}{1 0 \bar {\varrho}} \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {k + 1} - \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {k} + \frac {8}{\bar {\varrho}} \left(\frac {\hat {b} _ {k - 1}}{\hat {b} _ {k}} - \frac {\hat {b} _ {k}}{\hat {b} _ {k + 1}}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {\hat {b} _ {k - 1} - \hat {b} _ {k}}{2} \| x _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {d.5} \\ \end{array}
$$

we can easily check from the definition of  $\bar{f}_k(x_k,y_k)$  that

$$
\left\| \nabla \mathcal {g} _ {k} \right\| - \left\| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \right\| \leq \hat {b} _ {k} \| x _ {k} \|.
$$

similar to (c.9) and (c.10) in the proof of theorem 5.3, by replacing  $f$  with  $\bar{f}_k$ ,  $\nabla \mathcal{g}_k$  with  $\nabla \bar{\mathcal{g}}_k$ ,  $\varrho$  with  $\bar{\varrho}$ ,  $\bar{\xi}$  with  $\bar{\vartheta}_k + \bar{\iota}$  respectively, we conclude that

$$
\left\| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \leq \left(2 k _ {2} \left(\bar {\vartheta} _ {k} + \bar {\iota} + l _ {2 2}\right) ^ {2}\right) \| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {1}{\bar {\vartheta} ^ {2}} + 2 k _ {2} l _ {1 2} ^ {2}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \| ^ {2}. \tag {d.6}
$$

since both  $p_k$  and  $\bar{\vartheta}_k$  are in the same order when  $k$  becomes large enough, it then follows from the definition of  $\hat{d}_1$  that  $\forall k \geq 1$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {2 k _ {2} \left(\bar {\vartheta} _ {k} + \bar {\iota} + l _ {2 2}\right) ^ {2}}{p _ {k} ^ {2}} = \frac {2 k _ {1} \left(\bar {\varrho} l _ {2 1} ^ {2} + \frac {1 6 \tau l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{\bar {\varrho} (\bar {b} _ {k + 1}) ^ {2}} - \bar {\iota} + l _ {2 2}\right) ^ {2}}{p _ {k} ^ {2}} \\ \leq \frac {4 k _ {2} \left(\frac {1 6 \tau l _ {2 1} ^ {2}}{\bar {\varrho} (\hat {b} _ {k + 1}) ^ {2}}\right) ^ {2} + 4 k _ {2} \left(\bar {\varrho} l _ {2 1} ^ {2} - \bar {\iota} + l _ {2 2}\right) ^ {2}}{p _ {k} ^ {2}} \\ = \frac {1 6 k _ {2} \tau^ {2}}{(\tau - 2) ^ {2}} + \frac {1 9 ^ {4} \left(4 k _ {2} \left(\bar {\varrho} l _ {2 1} ^ {2} - \bar {\tau} + l _ {2 2}\right) ^ {2}\right)}{6 4 \cdot 2 0 ^ {4} \bar {\varrho} ^ {2} (\tau - 2) ^ {2} l _ {2 1} ^ {4} k} \\ \leq \hat {d} _ {1}. \tag {d.7} \\ \end{array}
$$

combining the previous two inequalities in (d.6) and (d.7), we obtain

$$
\left\| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \leq \hat {d} _ {1} \left(p _ {k}\right) ^ {2} \left\| y _ {k + 1} - y _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {1}{\bar {\varrho} ^ {2}} + 2 k _ {2} l _ {1 2} ^ {2}\right) \left\| x _ {k + 1} - x _ {k} \right\| ^ {2}. \tag {d.8}
$$

denote  $\hat{d}_k^{(2)} = \frac{1}{\max\left\{\hat{d}_1p_k,\frac{10 + 20k_2\bar{\varrho}^2l_{12}^2}{9\bar{\varrho}}\right\}}$  . by multiplying  $\hat{d}_k^{(2)}$  on the both sides of (d.8), and using (d.5), we get

$$
\hat {d} _ {k} ^ {(2)} \| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {k + 1} - \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {k} + \frac {8}{\bar {\varrho}} \left(\frac {\hat {b} _ {k - 1}}{\hat {b} _ {k}} - \frac {\hat {b} _ {k}}{\hat {b} _ {k + 1}}\right) \| x _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {b} _ {k - 1} - \hat {b} _ {k}}{2} \| x _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}. \tag {d.9}
$$

denoting

$$
\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon) := \min  \left\{k \mid \| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} \left(x _ {k}, y _ {k}\right) \| \leq \frac {\varepsilon}{2}, k \geq 2 \right\},
$$

summing both sides of (d.9) from  $k = 2$  to  $k = \bar{\mathcal{t}}(\varepsilon)$ , we then obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)} \hat {d} _ {k} ^ {(2)} \| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon) + 1} - \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {2} + \frac {8}{\bar {\varrho}} \left(\frac {\hat {b} _ {1}}{\hat {b} _ {2}} - \frac {\hat {b} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)}}{\hat {b} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon) + 1}}\right) \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {b} _ {1} - \hat {b} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)}}{2} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2} \\ \leq \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon) + 1} - \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {2} + \frac {8 \hat {b} _ {1}}{\bar {\varrho} \hat {b} _ {2}} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2} + \frac {\hat {b} _ {1}}{2} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2} \\ = \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon) + 1} - \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {2} + \frac {8 \cdot 2 ^ {1 / 4}}{\bar {\varrho}} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2} + \frac {1 9}{4 0 \bar {\varrho}} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2}. \tag {d.10} \\ \end{array}
$$

note that by the definition of  $\hat{\mathcal{f}}_{k + 1}$  in lemma d.3, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon) + 1} \leq \hat {l} + \frac {8 \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2}}{\bar {\varrho}} \left(1 + \frac {1}{\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)}\right) ^ {1 / 4} + \frac {1 5 \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2}}{\bar {\varrho}} + \frac {q _ {1} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2}}{2} \\ = \hat {l} + \left(2 ^ {1 3 / 4} + 1 5 + \frac {1 9}{4 0}\right) \frac {\hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2}}{\bar {\varrho}} = \hat {\mathcal {l}} _ {0}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\hat{l} \coloneqq \max_{(x,y) \in \mathcal{x} \times \mathcal{y}} l(x,y)$ . by the definition of  $\hat{\mathcal{l}}_2$ , we then conclude from (d.10) that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon)} \hat {d} _ {k} ^ {(2)} \| \nabla \bar {\mathcal {g}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq \hat {\mathcal {l}} _ {0} - \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {2} + \frac {8 \cdot 2 ^ {1 / 4}}{\bar {\varrho}} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2} + \frac {1 9}{4 0 \bar {\varrho}} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2} \\ \leq \hat {\mathcal {l}} _ {0} - \hat {\mathcal {l}} _ {2} + \frac {8 \cdot 2 ^ {1 / 4}}{\bar {\varrho}} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2} + \frac {1 9}{4 0 \bar {\varrho}} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {2} = \hat {r} _ {2}. \tag {d.11} \\ \end{array}
$$

note that  $\hat{r}_3 = \max \{\hat{d}_1, \frac{10 + 20k_2\bar{\varrho}^2l_{12}^2}{9\bar{\varrho}p_2}\}$ . since  $p_k$  is an increasing sequence when  $k \geq 2$ , we have that  $\hat{r}_3 \geq \max \{\hat{d}_1, \frac{10 + 20\bar{\varrho}^2l_{12}^2}{9\bar{\varrho}p_k}\}$  which implies that  $\hat{d}_k^{(2)} \geq \frac{1}{\hat{r}_3p_k}$ . by multiplying  $\hat{r}_3$  on the both sides of (d.11), and using the definition of  $\hat{r}_2$ , we have  $\sum_{k=2}^{\bar{t}(\varepsilon)} \frac{1}{p_k} \| \nabla \bar{\mathcal{g}}_k \|^2 \leq \hat{r}_2 \hat{r}_3$ , which, by the definition of  $\bar{t}(\varepsilon)$ , implies that

$$
\frac {\varepsilon^ {2}}{4} \leq \frac {\hat {r} _ {2} \hat {r} _ {3}}{\sum_ {k = 2} ^ {\bar {t} (\varepsilon)} \frac {1}{p _ {k}}}. \tag {d.12}
$$

using the assumptions  $\hat{b}_k = \frac{19}{20\bar{\varrho}k^{1/4}}$  and  $p_k = \frac{2\cdot 40^2\bar{\varrho}(\tau - 2)l_{21}^2\sqrt{k + 1}}{19^2}$ , (d.12) and the fact  $\sum_{k=2}^{\bar{t}(\varepsilon)} 1/\sqrt{k + 1} \geq \sqrt{\bar{t}(\varepsilon)} - 2$ , we conclude that  $\frac{\varepsilon^2}{4} \leq \frac{2\cdot 40^2\bar{\varrho}(\tau - 2)l_{21}^2\hat{r}_2\hat{r}_3}{19^2\left(\sqrt{\bar{t}(\varepsilon)} - 2\right)}$  or equivalently,

$$
\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon) \leq \left(\frac {2 \cdot 8 0 ^ {2} \bar {\varrho} (\tau - 2) l _ {2 1} ^ {2} \hat {r} _ {2} \hat {r} _ {3}}{1 9 ^ {2} \varepsilon^ {2}} + 2\right) ^ {2}.
$$

on the other hand, if  $k > \frac{19^4\delta_x^4}{10^4\bar{\varrho}^4\varepsilon^4}$ , then  $q_{k} = \frac{19}{20\bar{\varrho}k^{1 / 4}}\leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2\delta_{x}}$ , this inequality together with the definition of  $\hat{\delta}_x$  then imply that  $\hat{b}_{k}\| x_{k}\| \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2}$ . therefore, there exists a

$$
\mathcal {t} (\varepsilon) \leq \max \left(\bar {\mathcal {t}} (\varepsilon), \frac {1 9 ^ {4} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {4}}{1 0 ^ {4} \bar {\varrho} ^ {4} \varepsilon^ {4}}\right) \leq \max \left(\left(\frac {2 \cdot 8 0 ^ {2} \bar {\varrho} (\tau - 2) l _ {2 1} ^ {2} \hat {r} _ {2} \hat {r} _ {3}}{1 9 ^ {2} \varepsilon^ {2}} + 2\right) ^ {2}, \frac {1 9 ^ {4} \hat {\delta} _ {x} ^ {4}}{1 0 ^ {4} \bar {\varrho} ^ {4} \varepsilon^ {4}}\right),
$$

such that  $\| \nabla \mathcal{g}_k\| \leq \| \nabla \bar{\mathcal{g}}_k\| +\hat{b}_k\| x_k\| \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2} +\frac{\varepsilon}{2} = \varepsilon$
